The empirical results are compelling, and the problem is important. However, there are concerns on how to improve generalizability due to its sensitiveness towards data properties.

The key strength lies in the practical improvement it offers over existing NAT models for peptide sequencing, and the insights it generates around structured curriculum learning. The lack of deep theoretical analysis and relatively incremental innovation, particularly regarding the underlying transformer architecture, prevent it from achieving a higher score.

The influence of this work will likely be seen in improved peptide sequencing pipelines and as a starting point for future research exploring curriculum learning techniques for NAT models in bioinformatics.

- **Classification**: q-bio.BM
- **Score**: 7/10

### Watermarking LLM-Generated Datasets in Downstream Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13494v1)
- **Authors**: Yugeng Liu, Tianshuo Cong, Michael Backes, Zheng Li, Yang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have experienced rapid advancements, with applications spanning a wide range of fields, including sentiment classification, review generation, and question answering. Due to their efficiency and versatility, researchers and companies increasingly employ LLM-generated data to train their models. However, the inability to track content produced by LLMs poses a significant challenge, potentially leading to copyright infringement for the LLM owners. In this paper, we propose a method for injecting watermarks into LLM-generated datasets, enabling the tracking of downstream tasks to detect whether these datasets were produced using the original LLM. These downstream tasks can be divided into two categories. The first involves using the generated datasets at the input level, commonly for training classification tasks. The other is the output level, where model trainers use LLM-generated content as output for downstream tasks, such as question-answering tasks. We design a comprehensive set of experiments to evaluate both watermark methods. Our results indicate the high effectiveness of our watermark approach. Additionally, regarding model utility, we find that classifiers trained on the generated datasets achieve a test accuracy exceeding 0.900 in many cases, suggesting that the utility of such models remains robust. For the output-level watermark, we observe that the quality of the generated text is comparable to that produced using real-world datasets. Through our research, we aim to advance the protection of LLM copyrights, taking a significant step forward in safeguarding intellectual property in this domain.
- **Summary**: This paper proposes methods for watermarking LLM-generated datasets to track their usage in downstream tasks, addressing the challenge of copyright infringement for LLM owners. The approach involves injecting watermarks at both the input and output levels. For input-level tasks (e.g., classification), the authors explore trigger-based watermarks, including traditional word triggers and more stealthy stylistic triggers (poetry). For output-level tasks (e.g., generation), they investigate weak watermarks (based on soft prompting with green-red lists), robust watermarks (constraining the green list and using system prompts), and steganographic watermarks (using grammatical modifications). The paper presents extensive experiments across various LLMs (Llama, Ministral), datasets (AG News, DBpedia, IMDb, DialogSum), and downstream models (BERT, RoBERTa, T5, Qwen, Vicuna). The results indicate high effectiveness for input-level watermarks, especially with stylistic triggers, and varying degrees of effectiveness for output-level watermarks, with steganographic watermarks showing promise. The paper also evaluates the impact of watermark removal techniques (fine-tuning, pruning, quantization) on watermark robustness and model utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its comprehensive framework for watermarking LLM-generated datasets specifically *for downstream usage monitoring*. While individual watermarking techniques exist, the systematic approach to both input-level and output-level scenarios, the exploration of stylistic triggers and steganographic approaches in this context, and the evaluation of watermark removal techniques represent a valuable contribution. The idea of directly influencing generated datasets in the creation stage, rather than embedding watermarks within the weights of other models, is new. However, some of the individual watermarking strategies (e.g., green/red lists) are adapted from existing work. The application of steganography and style transfer in a watermark context, while mentioned in other works, is still relatively new in this specific problem.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a critical and growing concern: protecting the intellectual property rights of LLM providers in the age of widespread model fine-tuning.  The ability to track the usage of LLM-generated data is vital for enforcing copyright and preventing unauthorized model development. The paper provides a practical framework and concrete methods that can be used by LLM owners to monitor and potentially deter unauthorized usage.  The experiments provide valuable insights into the trade-offs between watermark effectiveness, model utility, and robustness against removal techniques. The research findings contribute to a better understanding of the challenges and opportunities in this domain. The focus on watermark detection even without access to model training data is an important contribution.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The paper provides a well-defined framework for watermarking LLM-generated datasets in downstream tasks, considering both input-level and output-level scenarios.
*   **Extensive Experiments:** The thorough experimental evaluation across diverse models, datasets, and scenarios provides strong empirical support for the proposed methods.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The paper addresses a practical and timely problem of copyright protection for LLMs.
*   **Evaluation of Removal Techniques:** The evaluation of fine-tuning, pruning, and quantization as watermark removal techniques provides valuable insights into the robustness of the proposed methods.
*   Addresses the lack of previous research on tracking LLM-generated *datasets*.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Adversarial Analysis:** While the paper evaluates watermark removal techniques, a more in-depth adversarial analysis, including sophisticated paraphrasing or obfuscation attacks, would strengthen the robustness evaluation. The methods could also look into adversarial training.
*   **Generalizability of Stilistic Watermarks:** The effectiveness of stylistic triggers (poetry) might be limited to specific domains or languages, which warrants further investigation. The reliance on stylistic information makes the model less stealthy.
*   **Output-Level Weak and Robust Watermarks:** The weakness of the output-level weak watermark and robust watermark methods raises concerns about their practical applicability. More work must be done on how this can be improved. The model would need to be more robust against prompt injection attacks, and the method has to be stealthy, which it is not currently.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a meaningful contribution to the field of LLM security by proposing a comprehensive framework for watermarking LLM-generated datasets and evaluating its effectiveness against various downstream tasks and removal techniques. The work is reasonably novel and addresses a pressing concern in the LLM ecosystem. However, the weaknesses regarding adversarial analysis, the generalizability of the stylistic watermark, and the output level method impact its overall significance. The paper is thorough but leaves certain aspects that can be improved in the future.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### BOW: Bottlenecked Next Word Exploration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13502v1)
- **Authors**: Ming Shen, Zhikun Xu, Xiao Ye, Jacob Dineen, Ben Zhou
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are typically trained via next-word prediction (NWP), which provides strong surface-level fluency but often lacks support for robust reasoning. We propose BOttlenecked next Word exploration (BOW), a novel RL framework that rethinks NWP by introducing a reasoning bottleneck where a policy model first generates a reasoning path rather than predicting the next token directly, after which a frozen judge model predicts the next token distribution based solely on this reasoning path. We train the policy model using GRPO with rewards that quantify how effectively the reasoning path facilitates next-word recovery. Compared with other continual pretraining baselines, we show that BOW improves both the general and next-word reasoning capabilities of the base model, evaluated on various benchmarks. Our findings show that BOW can serve as an effective and scalable alternative to vanilla NWP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Bottlenecked Next Word Exploration (BOW), a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework for training large language models (LLMs).  Instead of the standard next-word prediction (NWP) approach that directly supervises the model with the gold next word, BOW inserts a "reasoning bottleneck."  A policy model first generates a reasoning trajectory describing plausible next words, *without* seeing the gold word.  A separate, frozen "judge" model then predicts the next-token probability distribution solely based on this reasoning trajectory.  The policy model is trained via GRPO with rewards quantifying how well the reasoning path facilitates next-word recovery by the judge.  The authors demonstrate that BOW improves both general and next-word reasoning capabilities compared to continual pretraining baselines across various benchmarks. A new regularization technique prevents the model from collapsing into generating a small set of specific words.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of introducing a reasoning bottleneck via an RL framework is genuinely novel. The framework effectively disentangles the reasoning process from the direct prediction task, potentially encouraging more robust and interpretable reasoning. The regularization technique addressing model collapse is also a practical and innovative addition. However,  "reasoning bottlenecks" have been explored in other contexts. The core novelty lies in its *specific implementation* within the NWP task, using RL, a judge model, and the GRPO optimization. Prior approaches, such as ToW and QuietSTaR, share the high-level goal of encouraging reasoning, but BOW tackles this problem from an RL perspective, creating a self-evolving loop.

*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from addressing a key limitation of standard LLMs: their tendency to rely on surface correlations rather than genuine reasoning. BOW represents a promising approach to encourage LLMs to internalize deeper understanding and justification for language use.  The empirical results, demonstrating improved performance on reasoning benchmarks, provide compelling evidence for the effectiveness of the approach. If the method proves scalable, it could become an important alternative to vanilla NWP for training more robust and reliable LLMs. Additionally, the analysis into the types of reasoning paths generated by BOW offer valuable insights into how LLMs can be encouraged to reason more effectively. The study’s investigation into the “judge” model’s selection and its effect is useful.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of superficial correlation learning in standard NWP.
    *   **Innovative Framework:** The BOW framework is well-designed, logically sound, and addresses the identified problem.
    *   **Solid Empirical Results:** The experimental results provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of BOW, with improvements demonstrated across multiple benchmarks and policy models.
    *   **Ablation Studies and Human Analysis:** The thorough ablation studies and human analysis provide valuable insights into the contributions of different components of the BOW framework, as well as the nature of reasoning paths it generates.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the proposed framework, experimental setup, and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on a High-Quality Judge:** The performance of BOW heavily relies on the quality of the judge model. The paper acknowledges this limitation and shows how a biased judge (Qwen) can impact results. This raises concerns about the robustness and generalizability of the approach if a suitable judge model is unavailable.
    *   **Limited Generative Evaluation:**  The evaluations are largely focused on multiple-choice questions that are reformulated for NWP, rather than open-ended generation tasks. While this simplifies evaluation, it limits the assessment of BOW's ability to improve the quality and coherence of generated text in more complex scenarios.  It could be interesting to explore the effect of BOW on the generated texts directly.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** While the paper claims BOW is scalable, the experiments are conducted on relatively smaller models (7B and 8B parameters).  The computational cost of RL training and generating reasoning trajectories for each token during pretraining might pose a significant barrier to scaling BOW to larger models with trillions of parameters. What if, as models scaled up, the differences between the judge model and the RL model were reduced?
    *   **Task-Specific Tuning:** BOW likely requires careful task-specific prompt engineering and reward tuning to achieve optimal performance. There is a mention that it relies on carefully structured reasoning prompts. This could limit its applicability to broader domains without substantial effort.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper is likely to influence future research on training LLMs to improve their reasoning abilities.  The idea of inserting reasoning bottlenecks is a promising direction that could inspire other approaches. The framework and implementation details will be useful for researchers interested in exploring RL for language model training.

*   **Conclusion:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to improving the reasoning capabilities of LLMs. The innovative framework, solid empirical results, and insightful analysis make a valuable contribution to the field. While some limitations remain, BOW represents a significant step towards building more robust and interpretable language models.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale for Score:** The paper's novelty and significance are high, but the reliance on a high-quality judge and the limited generative evaluation detract from its overall impact. While it's an advance, the limitations and reliance on specific tunings prevent it from scoring higher. The framework is innovative and could have a substantial impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Safe-Child-LLM: A Developmental Benchmark for Evaluating LLM Safety in Child-AI Interactions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13510v1)
- **Authors**: Junfeng Jiao, Saleh Afroogh, Kevin Chen, Abhejay Murali, David Atkinson, Amit Dhurandhar
- **Abstract**: As Large Language Models (LLMs) increasingly power applications used by children and adolescents, ensuring safe and age-appropriate interactions has become an urgent ethical imperative. Despite progress in AI safety, current evaluations predominantly focus on adults, neglecting the unique vulnerabilities of minors engaging with generative AI. We introduce Safe-Child-LLM, a comprehensive benchmark and dataset for systematically assessing LLM safety across two developmental stages: children (7-12) and adolescents (13-17). Our framework includes a novel multi-part dataset of 200 adversarial prompts, curated from red-teaming corpora (e.g., SG-Bench, HarmBench), with human-annotated labels for jailbreak success and a standardized 0-5 ethical refusal scale. Evaluating leading LLMs -- including ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, LLaMA, DeepSeek, Grok, Vicuna, and Mistral -- we uncover critical safety deficiencies in child-facing scenarios. This work highlights the need for community-driven benchmarks to protect young users in LLM interactions. To promote transparency and collaborative advancement in ethical AI development, we are publicly releasing both our benchmark datasets and evaluation codebase at https://github.com/The-Responsible-AI-Initiative/Safe_Child_LLM_Benchmark.git
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Safe-Child-LLM," a benchmark and dataset specifically designed to evaluate the safety of large language models (LLMs) when interacting with children and adolescents. Recognizing that existing AI safety evaluations primarily focus on adults and neglect the unique vulnerabilities of minors, the authors curated a dataset of 200 adversarial prompts tailored to two developmental stages: children (7-12) and adolescents (13-17).  These prompts are derived from red-teaming corpora and are labeled for jailbreak success and ethical refusal.  The authors then evaluate several leading LLMs (ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, LLaMA, etc.) using this benchmark and highlight safety deficiencies in child-facing scenarios.  The paper emphasizes the need for community-driven benchmarks and provides open-source access to their datasets and evaluation code.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its specific focus on *child safety* in LLM interactions. While general AI safety benchmarks exist, they don't adequately address the nuanced developmental, emotional, and psychological considerations relevant to children. Creating age-specific prompts and a graded action labeling system is a valuable contribution. Moreover, the paper explicitly aims to go beyond binary "safe/unsafe" classifications by considering the *quality* of a model's refusal (or compliance). This is crucial, as a weak or ambiguous refusal can be just as harmful as explicit harmful content.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical ethical imperative. As LLMs become increasingly integrated into children's lives (education, entertainment, mental health), ensuring safe and age-appropriate interactions is paramount.  The benchmark provides a tangible tool for researchers and developers to assess and improve LLM safety in this context. By open-sourcing their dataset and evaluation code, the authors promote transparency, reproducibility, and community-driven advancements.  The paper's preliminary results demonstrating safety deficiencies in leading LLMs further underscores the importance of this work. The insights derived from this work can potentially inform policy decisions, development of child-specific safeguards, and responsible AI practices.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the "child safety gap" in existing LLM evaluation frameworks.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The creation of the age-specific dataset and the development of a graded action labeling system are well-explained.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The evaluation of multiple LLMs using the benchmark provides valuable insights into their safety performance.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:**  The open-source release of the dataset and evaluation code promotes transparency and collaborative advancement.
    *   **Addressing a Critical Need:** Directly tackles the ethical concerns related to child usage of LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The dataset, while carefully constructed, comprises only 200 prompts. A larger, more diverse dataset would strengthen the benchmark's robustness. The diversity of represented cultures within the prompt set might also need more explicit consideration.
    *   **General LLM Focus:** The paper focuses mainly on generic LLMs, but could explore the safety of LLMs which are specifically advertised for child-use, or those which specifically focus on children-centered activities, as those would be used more commonly by children/parents/schools than generic ones.
    *   **Human Annotation Bottleneck:**  Human annotation, while necessary for nuanced judgments, is time-consuming and potentially subjective.  The paper would benefit from discussing the inter-annotator agreement and the methods used to mitigate bias. While the taxonomy for rating has been identified, it is only vaguely mentioned, a more concise description would be beneficial.
    *   **Missing Contextual Analysis:** While the action label attempts to classify the response, a deeper contextual analysis that examines the child's potential interpretation of a given response may prove more useful.
    *   **Potential Misuse Concerns:**  While the authors acknowledge potential misuse of adversarial prompts, the paper would benefit from more detailed discussion on how to mitigate potential harms associated with disseminating such information, especially given the target audience is children.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to spur further research in child-centered AI safety evaluation. It will likely encourage the development of more robust and nuanced benchmarks, inform the design of child-specific safety filters, and shape responsible AI practices for applications used by children.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by specifically addressing the critical, and often overlooked, issue of child safety in LLM interactions. The novel benchmark, with its age-specific prompts and nuanced action labeling system, provides a valuable tool for researchers and developers.  The open-source nature of the work further enhances its potential impact.  However, limitations in dataset size, reliance on human annotation, and lack of a discussion of mitigations for potential misuse of adversarial prompts slightly temper the overall assessment. Despite these limitations, the paper's focus on a pressing ethical challenge and its practical contribution warrant a score of 7.5. This indicates a solid and important contribution to the field, with potential for future development and broader impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### TensorSLM: Energy-efficient Embedding Compression of Sub-billion Parameter Language Models on Low-end Devices
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13514v1)
- **Authors**: Mingxue Xu, Yao Lei Xu, Danilo P. Mandic
- **Abstract**: Small Language Models (SLMs, or on-device LMs) have significantly fewer parameters than Large Language Models (LLMs). They are typically deployed on low-end devices, like mobile phones and single-board computers. Unlike LLMs, which rely on increasing model size for better generalisation, SLMs designed for edge applications are expected to have adaptivity to the deployment environments and energy efficiency given the device battery life constraints, which are not addressed in datacenter-deployed LLMs. This paper addresses these two requirements by proposing a training-free token embedding compression approach using Tensor-Train Decomposition (TTD). Each pre-trained token embedding vector is converted into a lower-dimensional Matrix Product State (MPS). We comprehensively evaluate the extracted low-rank structures across compression ratio, language task performance, latency, and energy consumption on a typical low-end device, i.e. Raspberry Pi. Taking the sub-billion parameter versions of GPT-2/Cerebres-GPT and OPT models as examples, our approach achieves a comparable language task performance to the original model with around $2.0\times$ embedding layer compression, while the energy consumption of a single query drops by half.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "TensorSLM: Energy-efficient Embedding Compression of Sub-billion Parameter Language Models on Low-end Devices" proposes a training-free approach called TensorSLM for compressing the token embedding layer of small language models (SLMs) deployed on low-end devices like Raspberry Pi. TensorSLM uses Tensor-Train Decomposition (TTD) to convert pre-trained token embedding vectors into a lower-dimensional Matrix Product State (MPS), reducing memory footprint and energy consumption without significant performance degradation. The authors evaluate TensorSLM on GPT-2, CerebrasGPT, and OPT models, demonstrating comparable language task performance and halved energy consumption per query compared to the original models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of applying tensor decomposition, specifically TTD, to compress embedding layers isn't entirely novel. However, the paper's focus on *training-free* compression specifically for low-end devices and addressing the unique constraints of edge deployment adds a meaningful twist. The analysis of the trade-offs between compression ratio, task performance, latency, and energy consumption in this specific context is also valuable. The adaptation of TTD for a non-parallel forward pass is also a technical contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical challenge: deploying SLMs on resource-constrained devices. The energy efficiency focus is particularly relevant for mobile and edge applications. The empirical results demonstrate tangible benefits (halved energy consumption, comparable task performance) on real hardware (Raspberry Pi). This makes the work potentially impactful for practitioners aiming to deploy SLMs in real-world scenarios. The paper's exploration of the relationship between tensor structures and reasoning task performance provides a potential direction for further research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the specific challenges of deploying SLMs on low-end devices, emphasizing energy efficiency and adaptability.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The work is strongly grounded in real-world deployment considerations. The use of Raspberry Pi as a test platform adds credibility.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation covers various aspects: compression ratio, perplexity, sentiment classification, zero-shot reasoning, latency, and energy consumption.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:**  The paper compares TensorSLM to existing methods (e.g., SVD-based compression), highlighting its strengths and weaknesses.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Core Technique:** TTD itself is a well-established technique. The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application and the specific problem context.
    *   **Limited Model Variety:** While the paper tests on multiple GPT models, further validation with other SLM architectures would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Limited Scenarios:** It might have been stronger with an actual edge computing scenario example instead of simple benchmarks.
    *   **Complexity (TTD):** TTD adds complexity of implementation. The paper mentions some added latency that might not be trivial for highly responsive applications.

*   **Influence:** The paper could influence the design and deployment strategies for SLMs on edge devices. It highlights the importance of training-free compression techniques and the potential of tensor-based methods for achieving energy efficiency without sacrificing task performance. The insights on tensor structures and reasoning could inspire further research on model compression tailored to specific tasks.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the points above, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of SLM deployment on low-end devices. It tackles a practical problem with a focused approach and provides empirical evidence of its effectiveness. The limitations, such as the partial novelty of core algorithms and the limited model variety, prevent a higher score. A score of **7** seems appropriate, reflecting the paper's solid contribution, practical significance, and potential impact while acknowledging its shortcomings.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Seismic Acoustic Impedance Inversion Framework Based on Conditional Latent Generative Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13529v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Chen, Hongling Chen, Jinghuai Gao, Chuangji Meng, Tao Yang, XinXin Liang
- **Abstract**: Seismic acoustic impedance plays a crucial role in lithological identification and subsurface structure interpretation. However, due to the inherently ill-posed nature of the inversion problem, directly estimating impedance from post-stack seismic data remains highly challenging. Recently, diffusion models have shown great potential in addressing such inverse problems due to their strong prior learning and generative capabilities. Nevertheless, most existing methods operate in the pixel domain and require multiple iterations, limiting their applicability to field data. To alleviate these limitations, we propose a novel seismic acoustic impedance inversion framework based on a conditional latent generative diffusion model, where the inversion process is made in latent space. To avoid introducing additional training overhead when embedding conditional inputs, we design a lightweight wavelet-based module into the framework to project seismic data and reuse an encoder trained on impedance to embed low-frequency impedance into the latent space. Furthermore, we propose a model-driven sampling strategy during the inversion process of this framework to enhance accuracy and reduce the number of required diffusion steps. Numerical experiments on a synthetic model demonstrate that the proposed method achieves high inversion accuracy and strong generalization capability within only a few diffusion steps. Moreover, application to field data reveals enhanced geological detail and higher consistency with well-log measurements, validating the effectiveness and practicality of the proposed approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel seismic acoustic impedance inversion framework called SAII-CLDM, based on a conditional latent generative diffusion model. It addresses the limitations of existing diffusion-based methods by performing inversion in a lower-dimensional latent space for efficiency.  It employs a wavelet-based module (SHWT) to project seismic data into the latent space, reusing a pre-trained VQ-GAN encoder for low-frequency impedance. A model-driven sampling strategy is proposed to accelerate the inversion process and improve accuracy. The authors validate the framework through synthetic and field data experiments, comparing it against supervised learning (SDL), unsupervised learning (USDL), and Total Variation (2D-TV) inversion techniques. Results show improved inversion accuracy, robustness to noise, and generalization capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits moderate novelty. The core idea of applying diffusion models to seismic inversion is not entirely new, as the authors acknowledge prior work, including their own.  The key novelties lie in:

    *   **Latent Space Inversion:**  Performing the diffusion process in a lower-dimensional latent space is a significant contribution to efficiency and scalability, directly addressing a known limitation of pixel-space diffusion models.  This is a crucial step towards making diffusion models practical for real-world, large-scale seismic data.

    *   **Wavelet-Based Module (SHWT):** The SHWT module for projecting seismic data into the latent space is a lightweight and elegant solution to incorporate conditional information without introducing significant training overhead. The reuse of the VQ-GAN encoder is also a clever approach.

    *   **Model-Driven Sampling:** Integrating forward modeling constraints into the sampling process (model-driven sampling) to enhance robustness to distribution shifts is a valuable improvement, particularly relevant in geophysical applications where data distributions can vary substantially.

*   **Significance:** The paper holds considerable significance for the seismic inversion community.

    *   **Addressing Ill-Posedness:** Seismic inversion is inherently ill-posed, and the proposed framework tackles this by leveraging the strong prior learning capabilities of diffusion models.
    *   **Improved Performance:**  The experimental results (both synthetic and field data) demonstrate tangible improvements in inversion accuracy, noise robustness, and the ability to resolve fine-scale geological features compared to established techniques (SDL, USDL, 2D-TV).  The acceleration achieved by the model-driven sampling strategy is also significant.
    *   **Scalability:**  The latent space approach addresses the computational burden of diffusion models, paving the way for applications to larger 3D seismic datasets.
    *   **Limitations/Weaknesses:**
        *   The experiments, while comprehensive, could benefit from additional real-world field datasets with more extensive well-log control for validation.
        *   The parameter tuning and sensitivity analysis of hyperparameters such as the trade-off parameter γ in the stochastic resampling are not sufficiently explored.
        *   Discussion of the limitations related to specific geological settings or types of noise.
        *   The reliance on the quality of the pre-trained VQ-GAN encoder should be further addressed.
        *   Ablation studies for the various components (SHWT, model-driven sampling) to quantify their individual contributions are lacking.

*   **Impact:** The paper has a good potential for impact.  The combination of latent space diffusion, a tailored wavelet transform, and model-driven sampling provides a promising pathway for more accurate and efficient seismic inversion. Wider adoption depends on how well the framework generalizes to diverse geological settings and data acquisition scenarios and how easily it can be integrated into existing seismic processing workflows. The code availability is important for fostering broader use.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7.5**. The paper is a valuable contribution with tangible improvements over existing techniques, addressing a critical need in seismic inversion. The combination of latent space diffusion, SHWT, and model-driven sampling demonstrates strong engineering and a clear understanding of the geophysical problem. However, some limitations in the analysis and a need for further validation prevent it from achieving a higher score. The lack of certain ablation studies and more in-depth sensitivity analyses also limit the scope of its contribution. The paper is a substantial step forward, but with further work, it could achieve even greater impact.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### X-Scene: Large-Scale Driving Scene Generation with High Fidelity and Flexible Controllability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13558v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Yang, Alan Liang, Jianbiao Mei, Yukai Ma, Yong Liu, Gim Hee Lee
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models are advancing autonomous driving by enabling realistic data synthesis, predictive end-to-end planning, and closed-loop simulation, with a primary focus on temporally consistent generation. However, the generation of large-scale 3D scenes that require spatial coherence remains underexplored. In this paper, we propose X-Scene, a novel framework for large-scale driving scene generation that achieves both geometric intricacy and appearance fidelity, while offering flexible controllability. Specifically, X-Scene supports multi-granular control, including low-level conditions such as user-provided or text-driven layout for detailed scene composition and high-level semantic guidance such as user-intent and LLM-enriched text prompts for efficient customization. To enhance geometrical and visual fidelity, we introduce a unified pipeline that sequentially generates 3D semantic occupancy and the corresponding multiview images, while ensuring alignment between modalities. Additionally, we extend the generated local region into a large-scale scene through consistency-aware scene outpainting, which extrapolates new occupancy and images conditioned on the previously generated area, enhancing spatial continuity and preserving visual coherence. The resulting scenes are lifted into high-quality 3DGS representations, supporting diverse applications such as scene exploration. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that X-Scene significantly advances controllability and fidelity for large-scale driving scene generation, empowering data generation and simulation for autonomous driving.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "X-Scene: Large-Scale Driving Scene Generation with High Fidelity and Flexible Controllability."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces X-Scene, a novel framework for generating large-scale 3D driving scenes.  It aims to address the limitations of existing methods by achieving both geometric intricacy, photorealistic appearance, and flexible controllability through multi-granular control (low-level layout and high-level semantic guidance) and consistency across large scenes. X-Scene employs a unified pipeline for sequentially generating 3D semantic occupancy and corresponding multi-view images, enhanced by a consistency-aware scene outpainting mechanism to extrapolate scenes while preserving spatial continuity. The final scenes are represented using 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) for downstream applications like driving simulation and scene exploration. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of X-Scene through comprehensive experiments, showcasing improvements in generation quality, controllability, and scalability compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core contribution lies in the *combination* of several techniques and their integration into a unified, controllable, and scalable driving scene generation framework. While individual components such as diffusion models, 3DGS representations, and outpainting techniques are not entirely novel in isolation, their specific combination and application within the context of large-scale driving scene generation presents a significant advancement.
    *   **Strength:** The multi-granular control mechanism effectively bridges the gap between precise, layout-driven scene construction and intuitive, prompt-driven customization. The consistency-aware outpainting technique represents a valuable contribution to maintaining spatial coherence across extended scenes. The integrated pipeline that handles 3D occupancy and multi-view image generation to ensure modal alignment is a strong asset.
    *   **Weakness:** The paper may be considered incremental in that it builds upon existing methodologies. A major leap in the individual core technologies is not demonstrated, but instead an improved combination, adaptation, and management of techniques. The dependence on pretrained models (Stable Diffusion, QWen, T5 encoder) somewhat constrains the novelty of the end-to-end system. It may also be hard to disentangle the impact of architectural contributions from the improvements gained by the use of modern LLMs.

*   **Significance:** This work has the potential to significantly impact the field of autonomous driving and simulation.
    *   **Strength:** High-fidelity and controllable scene generation is crucial for creating realistic training environments for autonomous vehicles. X-Scene's ability to generate large-scale, diverse, and complex driving scenarios could accelerate the development and validation of self-driving technologies. The downstream task evaluations (semantic occupancy prediction, 3D object detection, BEV segmentation) provide evidence of the practical utility of the generated scenes. The emphasis on cross-modal consistency (geometric and visual) makes the generated data more valuable for training perception systems.
    *   **Weakness:** The sim-to-real gap remains a challenge. While the paper demonstrates improved performance on downstream tasks using the generated data, the ultimate validation requires testing on real-world autonomous driving systems. The paper may not deeply explore the computational cost of training and inference, which can be a significant factor for practical adoption. The limited geometric and semantic diversity of the nuScenes dataset, as acknowledged in the paper, constrains the generalizability of the generated scenes, even with the LLM prompt system.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly structured. The figures and tables effectively illustrate the proposed framework and experimental results. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Strength:** The visual results are compelling and demonstrate the capabilities of X-Scene. The qualitative comparisons with existing methods clearly highlight the improvements in generation quality and consistency.
    *   **Weakness:** Some sections could benefit from further elaboration. For example, more details on the specific architecture and training procedure of the triplane deformable attention mechanism would be helpful. A more thorough discussion of the limitations and potential failure cases would also strengthen the paper.

*   **Reproducibility:** While the paper provides some implementation details, ensuring full reproducibility would require releasing the code and trained models. The system prompt for the LLM used to generate the description texts is provided.

**Overall:**

X-Scene presents a valuable contribution to the field of large-scale driving scene generation by combining existing techniques in a novel way to address the challenges of geometric fidelity, visual realism, and controllability. The paper has potential impact and opens up opportunities for future research in autonomous driving simulation. However, its reliance on pretrained models and limited dataset prevent greater perceived contribution.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The score reflects the novelty in its *integration* and adaptation of existing methods, the significance of the application domain, and the good experimental results. The relatively high score acknowledges the importance of the problem being addressed and the clear advancements made by the proposed framework. However, the lack of a complete paradigm shift, limited source code release, and existing dataset usage, prevents a score of 8 or higher. The critical elements addressed for driving scene generation are significant but require further development with more robust source code and diverse data sets to have a higher score.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Flexible-length Text Infilling for Discrete Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13579v1)
- **Authors**: Andrew Zhang, Anushka Sivakumar, Chiawei Tang, Chris Thomas
- **Abstract**: Discrete diffusion models are a new class of text generators that offer advantages such as bidirectional context use, parallelizable generation, and flexible prompting compared to autoregressive models. However, a critical limitation of discrete diffusion models is their inability to perform flexible-length or flexible-position text infilling without access to ground-truth positional data. We introduce \textbf{DDOT} (\textbf{D}iscrete \textbf{D}iffusion with \textbf{O}ptimal \textbf{T}ransport Position Coupling), the first discrete diffusion model to overcome this challenge. DDOT jointly denoises token values and token positions, employing a novel sample-level Optimal Transport (OT) coupling. This coupling preserves relative token ordering while dynamically adjusting the positions and length of infilled segments, a capability previously missing in text diffusion. Our method is orthogonal to existing discrete text diffusion methods and is compatible with various pretrained text denoisers. Extensive experiments on text infilling benchmarks such as One-Billion-Word and Yelp demonstrate that DDOT outperforms naive diffusion baselines. Furthermore, DDOT achieves performance on par with state-of-the-art non-autoregressive models and enables significant improvements in training efficiency and flexibility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Flexible-length Text Infilling for Discrete Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DDOT (Discrete Diffusion with Optimal Transport Position Coupling), a novel discrete diffusion model designed to overcome the limitation of existing discrete diffusion models: their inability to handle flexible-length or flexible-position text infilling without ground-truth positional data. DDOT achieves this by jointly denoising token values and token positions. It employs a sample-level Optimal Transport (OT) coupling to preserve relative token ordering while dynamically adjusting the positions and lengths of infilled segments. The method is orthogonal to existing discrete text diffusion approaches and compatible with various pre-trained text denoisers. Experiments on text infilling benchmarks demonstrate that DDOT outperforms naive diffusion baselines and achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art non-autoregressive models, while improving training efficiency and flexibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty of the paper lies in the application of OT for explicitly modeling and controlling token positions within a discrete diffusion framework for text infilling. Prior discrete diffusion models largely treated positions as fixed, limiting their flexibility. While OT has been used in image generation, applying it to text, and specifically for the infilling task to manage token order, is a substantial contribution. The coupling of discrete token values with continuous position variables via OT is also a novel approach.

*   **Significance:** DDOT addresses a critical limitation of existing discrete diffusion models, opening up new possibilities for text generation tasks that require flexible context and control. Enabling flexible-length infilling without requiring oracle positions is a significant advance. The fact that DDOT's approach is compatible with existing text denoisers further enhances its potential impact. However, the improvements over SOTA are only on par and not significantly better.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the DDOT framework and its advantages.
    *   The use of OT is theoretically well-motivated and practically effective, as evidenced by the experimental results.
    *   The experiments are thorough, covering various benchmarks and comparing against strong baselines. The ablations provide valuable insights into the importance of OT coupling.
    *   The approach is orthogonal to existing denoisers for token values, and provides flexible architecture.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While DDOT shows good results, its main advantage comes from improving diffusion baselines, rather than surpassing state-of-the-art approaches on the whole.
    *   The complexity of OT can be seen as a drawback. While the paper addresses computational efficiency, the framework still requires careful implementation and tuning of OT-related parameters.
    *   The reliance on SEDD might limit the architecture; the flexibility of the core framework may not be fully demonstrated with the current implementation.
    *   The limitations should be discussed in greater depth, specifically, more comprehensive analysis of parameters impacting model convergence.
    *   There are some formatting issues and inconsistencies throughout the paper (e.g., hyphenation errors in the abstract, inconsistent capitalization in "Implementation Details").

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more flexible and controllable text generation models.  DDOT's approach of explicitly modeling token positions with OT could be adopted in other sequence generation tasks beyond infilling. However, wider adoption will depend on its ease of implementation and its ability to scale to larger datasets and more complex models.

**Justification for Score:**

DDOT makes a novel and valuable contribution by addressing a key limitation of discrete diffusion models for text generation. The use of OT coupling is theoretically sound and practically effective. Although the gains might not be drastic compared to the SOTA, the significance of the improvement to diffusion baselines and the additional flexibility it brings to diffusion models warrant a high score. Overall, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a meaningful contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Omni-AdaVideoRAG: Omni-Contextual Adaptive Retrieval-Augmented for Efficient Long Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13589v1)
- **Authors**: Zhucun Xue, Jiangning Zhang, Xurong Xie, Yuxuan Cai, Yong Liu, Xiangtai Li, Dacheng Tao
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) struggle with long videos due to fixed context windows and weak long-term dependency modeling. Existing Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) methods for videos use static retrieval strategies, leading to inefficiencies for simple queries and information loss for complex tasks. To address this, we propose AdaVideoRAG, a novel framework that dynamically adapts retrieval granularity based on query complexity using a lightweight intent classifier. Our framework employs an Omni-Knowledge Indexing module to build hierarchical databases from text (captions, ASR, OCR), visual features, and semantic graphs, enabling optimal resource allocation across tasks. We also introduce the HiVU benchmark for comprehensive evaluation. Experiments demonstrate improved efficiency and accuracy for long-video understanding, with seamless integration into existing MLLMs. AdaVideoRAG establishes a new paradigm for adaptive retrieval in video analysis. Codes will be open-sourced at https://github.com/xzc-zju/AdaVideoRAG.
- **Summary**: The paper "AdaVideoRAG: Omni-Contextual Adaptive Retrieval-Augmented Efficient Long Video Understanding" introduces a novel framework, AdaVideoRAG, designed to improve the performance and efficiency of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in understanding long videos. It addresses the limitations of existing RAG approaches which use fixed retrieval paradigms, leading to redundancy and potential information loss. AdaVideoRAG employs a lightweight intent classifier to dynamically allocate appropriate retrieval schemes based on query complexity, ranging from simple naive retrieval to more sophisticated graph-based retrieval. The paper also proposes an Omni-Knowledge Indexing module that extracts valuable information from multi-modal signals (text, visual, and graph) to build corresponding databases. The method is evaluated using the introduced HiVU benchmark, demonstrating enhancements in accuracy and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects:
*   **Adaptive RAG framework for video:** This is the core contribution. The idea of dynamically adjusting the retrieval strategy based on query intent is promising for long-video understanding, which benefits from hierarchical knowledge extraction. While adaptive RAG has been explored in other contexts, applying it specifically to the unique challenges of long-video understanding with multi-modal data is novel.
*   **Omni-Knowledge Indexing Module:** The idea of indexing multi-modal signals (text, visual, and graph) into structured databases and retrieving knowledge hierarchically based on query complexity is novel.
*   **HiVU Benchmark:** The HiVU benchmark is a significant contribution as it introduces a three-level difficulty quantification system (Basic, Advanced, Expert) designed to evaluate the multi-level reasoning capabilities of video understanding models. This directly addresses the lack of appropriate evaluation data in the area.

**Significance:**
*   The AdaVideoRAG framework has the potential to improve the efficiency and accuracy of MLLMs in video understanding, particularly for long videos where context modeling and resource management are critical. It offers a more nuanced approach compared to existing RAG strategies.
*   The HiVU benchmark could become a valuable resource for the community, fostering further research and development in long-video understanding and reasoning. The multi-level query design addresses the inherent need for tiered evaluation criteria.
*   The paper presents a well-structured approach, integrating several components (intent classification, knowledge indexing, adaptive retrieval) into a cohesive framework.
* The extensive experimental results show effectiveness in multiple tasks.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on existing models:** The framework depends on existing MLLMs, ImageBind and Text embedding models. While this allows for integration, the overall system's performance is capped by the performance of these components. The paper does not propose innovation in the models themselves, rather in how they are used in an adaptive RAG system.
*   **Computational overhead for intent classification:** While claimed to be lightweight, the intent classification process introduces its own computational overhead. The paper should provide a more detailed analysis of the runtime cost and trade-off between complexity and efficiency to showcase the real-world practical applications of the AdaVideoRAG.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** The paper introduces the Win-Rate comparison which considers five dimensions. It is unclear how exactly the evaluation process is implemented or the judgement is quantified.
*   **Limited ablation study:** While some ablation experiments are presented, a more comprehensive analysis exploring the impact of each module (intent classifier, knowledge indexing, etc.) would be beneficial.
*   **Clarity and completeness of descriptions:** While the overall framework is explained, there are certain implementation details that could be more thoroughly described. For example, the exact steps for constructing the knowledge graph could be expanded to ensure reproducibility.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-engineered contribution to the field of long-video understanding. The adaptive RAG framework, combined with the Omni-Knowledge Indexing module and the HiVU benchmark, significantly advances the current state-of-the-art. While the approach relies on existing MLLMs and there is a level of computational overhead with intent classification, the demonstrated improvements in accuracy and efficiency, alongside the creation of a much needed dataset, warrant a high score. A more detailed discussion on deployment considerations would be welcome, however, the research provides a clear step towards improving MLLM capabilities.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Dive3D: Diverse Distillation-based Text-to-3D Generation via Score Implicit Matching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13594v1)
- **Authors**: Weimin Bai, Yubo Li, Wenzheng Chen, Weijian Luo, He Sun
- **Abstract**: Distilling pre-trained 2D diffusion models into 3D assets has driven remarkable advances in text-to-3D synthesis. However, existing methods typically rely on Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) loss, which involves asymmetric KL divergence--a formulation that inherently favors mode-seeking behavior and limits generation diversity. In this paper, we introduce Dive3D, a novel text-to-3D generation framework that replaces KL-based objectives with Score Implicit Matching (SIM) loss, a score-based objective that effectively mitigates mode collapse. Furthermore, Dive3D integrates both diffusion distillation and reward-guided optimization under a unified divergence perspective. Such reformulation, together with SIM loss, yields significantly more diverse 3D outputs while improving text alignment, human preference, and overall visual fidelity. We validate Dive3D across various 2D-to-3D prompts and find that it consistently outperforms prior methods in qualitative assessments, including diversity, photorealism, and aesthetic appeal. We further evaluate its performance on the GPTEval3D benchmark, comparing against nine state-of-the-art baselines. Dive3D also achieves strong results on quantitative metrics, including text-asset alignment, 3D plausibility, text-geometry consistency, texture quality, and geometric detail.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Dive3D paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Dive3D, a novel text-to-3D generation framework.  It addresses the limited diversity of existing methods, which primarily rely on Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) loss. Dive3D replaces the KL-based objectives in SDS with Score Implicit Matching (SIM) loss. This is claimed to mitigate mode collapse and improve generation diversity. Furthermore, Dive3D integrates both diffusion distillation and reward-guided optimization under a unified divergence perspective. The authors demonstrate that Dive3D generates more diverse, realistic, and semantically aligned 3D assets, outperforming existing methods qualitatively and quantitatively on the GPTEval3D benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in replacing KL-divergence-based losses with Score Implicit Matching (SIM) loss in the text-to-3D generation context. While SIM loss has been explored in other generative tasks (one-step diffusion, flow matching), its application to 3D asset generation, along with its integration into a unified divergence-based framework combining diffusion priors and reward signals, represents a significant contribution. The analysis of how existing SDS and reward guidance methods can be framed as KL divergence minimization is also a valuable insight.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation in text-to-3D generation: the lack of diversity. The authors provide a theoretical justification for this limitation by connecting it to the inherent mode-seeking behavior of KL divergence. The proposed Dive3D framework offers a practical solution that demonstrably improves diversity without sacrificing fidelity or text alignment. The experimental results on the GPTEval3D benchmark and the qualitative comparisons showcase the effectiveness of the approach.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides a clear and well-reasoned explanation for the diversity limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Unified Framework:** Integrating diffusion distillation and reward guidance within a unified divergence perspective is a conceptually elegant and practically effective approach.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Extensive qualitative and quantitative evaluations demonstrate the superiority of Dive3D over existing methods. The use of the GPTEval3D benchmark strengthens the credibility of the results.
    *   **Implementation Details:** The paper provides sufficient implementation details (in the supplementary material) to allow for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges that Dive3D's runtime is slower than LRM-based methods. This is a significant limitation, especially for practical applications. The authors propose integrating Dive3D with LRM techniques as future work, but this remains to be demonstrated.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** While the paper includes a section on the impact of divergence loss combinations, a more thorough ablation study exploring the sensitivity of the results to different hyperparameters (e.g., the weighting factor *λ* for the reward term) would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Incremental Improvement?** While the results are good, is the core novelty (SIM) a fundamental breakthrough or an incremental improvement upon existing techniques? While SIM has shown benefits elsewhere, its translation to text-to-3D generation is not necessarily a groundbreaking advance. There is a risk the method is good but not highly significant.
*   **Potential Influence:** Dive3D has the potential to influence future research in text-to-3D generation by shifting the focus from KL-divergence minimization to score-based divergence optimization. The framework's modular design could facilitate the integration of other advanced techniques, such as improved diffusion priors and reward models. The improved diversity of generated 3D assets could also unlock new applications in content creation and virtual reality.
*   **Risk:** The reliance on the GPTEval3D benchmark for quantitative assessment has some inherent risks, as these benchmarks are often subject to overfitting and may not fully capture all aspects of 3D generation quality.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the novelty of the approach, the significance of the problem addressed, the strengths of the empirical validation, and the potential influence on the field, but considering the computational cost and possibility of incremental improvement over the other SIM implementations, I assign a score of:

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Qwen vs. Gemma Integration with Whisper: A Comparative Study in Multilingual SpeechLLM Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13596v1)
- **Authors**: Tuan Nguyen, Long-Vu Hoang, Huy-Dat Tran
- **Abstract**: This paper presents our system for the MLC-SLM Challenge 2025, focusing on multilingual speech recognition and language modeling with large language models (LLMs). Our approach combines a fine-tuned Whisper-large-v3 encoder with efficient projector architectures and various decoder configurations. We employ a three-stage training methodology that progressively optimizes the encoder, projector, and LLM components. Our system achieves competitive performance with a private test average WER/CER result of 16.63% using the Gemma3-12B and 18.6% using the Qwen2.5-7B as decoder-only language model.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a system designed for the MLC-SLM Challenge 2025, focusing on multilingual speech recognition and language modeling. The core approach combines a fine-tuned Whisper-large-v3 encoder with different projector architectures and decoder configurations (Qwen2.5-7B and Gemma3-12B). The system employs a three-stage training methodology to progressively optimize the encoder, projector, and LLM components. The paper reports competitive performance on the private test set, with WER/CER results of 16.63% using Gemma3-12B and 18.6% using Qwen2.5-7B as the decoder. The paper includes ablation studies to analyze the contribution of each component and provides per-language results on the development set.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Architecture:** The system architecture is well-defined and modular, consisting of a pre-trained speech encoder (Whisper), a lightweight projector, and a large language model. This modularity enables flexible experimentation with different components.
    *   **Effective Training Methodology:** The three-stage training approach is a sensible strategy for optimizing each part of the system progressively, preventing any single component from dominating and destabilizing the training process.
    *   **Strong Baseline:** Using Whisper as a fixed speech encoder is a good starting point.  Whisper has proven robust across various languages and noisy conditions, simplifying the overall system training.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The inclusion of ablation studies provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component, allowing the authors (and readers) to understand the system's behavior.  The per-language results offer a more granular analysis than just aggregate WER/CER.
    *   **Competitive Results:** The results are competitive with the baseline and other systems, particularly with the Gemma3-12B integration.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty:** While the system achieves competitive performance, the overall architecture is relatively standard. The use of Whisper, a projector, and a pre-trained LLM is a common approach in the field. The paper lacks in-depth discussion or a specific novelty regarding the combination of the architecture's existing blocks.
    *   **Projector Design:** The description of the projector is very basic (two-layer perceptron with SwiGLU). Given the importance of aligning speech and text modalities, a more detailed investigation and justification of the projector design would have been valuable. A comparison with more sophisticated alignment techniques (e.g., cross-attention) could have significantly improved the paper's impact.
    *   **Lack of Deep Analysis:** The paper doesn't delve deeply into *why* certain language models perform better than others for speech recognition, or what specific properties of speech are effectively captured by the projector. There needs to be deeper analysis of the failure modes of the system.
    *   **Limited Scope of Experiments:** The experimentation feels somewhat limited. Only using the Whisper encoder in this setup restricts the scope to only decoder-only LLMs. In current SOTA multimodal speech recognition, the scope extends far beyond that with encoders, such as HuBERT and data2vec, which provide better performance on average.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper is a solid engineering effort that demonstrates the practical application of existing technologies. However, it does not significantly advance the state-of-the-art or present fundamentally new insights. The incremental improvements in performance are valuable, but they don't represent a breakthrough.
    *   The modular architecture and training methodology could be useful for other researchers and practitioners working in the field of multilingual speech recognition. The results provide a concrete example of how to effectively combine pre-trained models for this task.

**Justification for the Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **6**. Here's why:

The paper demonstrates a practical and well-executed approach to multilingual speech recognition using pre-trained models. It provides competitive results and valuable ablation studies. *However*, the lack of significant architectural novelty and in-depth analysis limits its overall impact. While the engineering is solid, it does not present a new fundamental understanding or technique that would significantly move the field forward.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### CAMS: A CityGPT-Powered Agentic Framework for Urban Human Mobility Simulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13599v1)
- **Authors**: Yuwei Du, Jie Feng, Jian Yuan, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Human mobility simulation plays a crucial role in various real-world applications. Recently, to address the limitations of traditional data-driven approaches, researchers have explored leveraging the commonsense knowledge and reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) to accelerate human mobility simulation. However, these methods suffer from several critical shortcomings, including inadequate modeling of urban spaces and poor integration with both individual mobility patterns and collective mobility distributions. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{C}ityGPT-Powered \textbf{A}gentic framework for \textbf{M}obility \textbf{S}imulation (\textbf{CAMS}), an agentic framework that leverages the language based urban foundation model to simulate human mobility in urban space. \textbf{CAMS} comprises three core modules, including MobExtractor to extract template mobility patterns and synthesize new ones based on user profiles, GeoGenerator to generate anchor points considering collective knowledge and generate candidate urban geospatial knowledge using an enhanced version of CityGPT, TrajEnhancer to retrieve spatial knowledge based on mobility patterns and generate trajectories with real trajectory preference alignment via DPO. Experiments on real-world datasets show that \textbf{CAMS} achieves superior performance without relying on externally provided geospatial information. Moreover, by holistically modeling both individual mobility patterns and collective mobility constraints, \textbf{CAMS} generates more realistic and plausible trajectories. In general, \textbf{CAMS} establishes a new paradigm that integrates the agentic framework with urban-knowledgeable LLMs for human mobility simulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CAMS, a CityGPT-Powered Agentic framework for urban human mobility simulation.  CAMS aims to improve upon existing methods that leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) by addressing shortcomings in urban space modeling and integration of individual and collective mobility patterns. The framework comprises three core modules: MobExtractor (extracts and synthesizes mobility patterns), GeoGenerator (generates anchor points using a CityGPT-enhanced model and collective knowledge), and TrajEnhancer (generates trajectories using mobility patterns and spatial knowledge, aligning with real trajectory preferences via Direct Preference Optimization - DPO). Experiments on real-world datasets show that CAMS achieves superior performance, generating more realistic and plausible trajectories without relying on external geospatial information. The paper positions CAMS as a new paradigm integrating agentic frameworks with urban-knowledgeable LLMs for human mobility simulation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its agentic framework that tightly integrates a specialized LLM (CityGPT) for urban spatial understanding with individual mobility pattern extraction and collective mobility constraints.  While prior work explored LLMs for mobility simulation, CAMS's focus on enhancing the LLM's *native* reasoning with urban spatial knowledge, its structured agent-based design and iterative refinement using DPO appears to be a novel contribution.  The dual-phase mobility pattern extraction and iterative refinement through DPO add incremental novelty. However, the individual components (CityGPT itself, DPO) aren't entirely novel in isolation.

*   **Significance:** Human mobility simulation is a relevant and important problem, as highlighted by the paper. Improving the realism, controllability, and spatial transferability of these simulations has practical implications for urban planning, transportation optimization, and epidemic control. If CAMS can indeed generate more realistic and controllable trajectories, it represents a significant advance. The reported superior performance over existing methods, especially without reliance on external geospatial information, supports this. The approach of incorporating urban spatial information into the LLM reasoning offers a more powerful way of handling mobility simulation, allowing the generation of more realistic urban trajectories.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined agentic framework with clear modularity (MobExtractor, GeoGenerator, TrajEnhancer).
    *   Integration of an urban foundation model (CityGPT) for spatial reasoning.
    *   Use of DPO for iterative refinement and spatiotemporal consistency.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with real-world datasets and comparisons to diverse baselines.
    *   Demonstrated improvements in trajectory generation, especially in spatial distribution metrics.
    *   Clear articulation of limitations in existing approaches and how CAMS addresses them.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty of the *individual* components might be limited (CityGPT enhancements, DPO adoption).
    *   The evaluation, while extensive, could benefit from qualitative analysis or user studies to confirm perceived realism.  Statistical metrics are useful, but human judgment is crucial for assessing plausibility.
    *   The paper could explore the computational costs of CAMS.  Agentic frameworks can be computationally intensive.
    *   The paper could also delve deeper into the limitations of the framework, particularly regarding handling extreme events or unforeseen circumstances that the LLM may not have been trained on.

*   **Potential Influence:** CAMS has the potential to influence future research in human mobility simulation by:
    *   Encouraging the use of specialized LLMs with native urban spatial knowledge.
    *   Promoting the agentic framework approach for better modularity and control.
    *   Providing a new benchmark for evaluating mobility simulation methods.
    *   Inspiring further research on iterative refinement techniques like DPO in this context.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, and after critical evaluation, I assign a score of 7.  While the individual components have partial precedence, the integrated *framework* with its specialized LLM and DPO loop contributes significant novelty. The improvements demonstrated in the experiments, and the potential for impact in urban planning, justify this score. However, the lack of qualitative evaluation and analysis of real-world impacts, coupled with potential computational limitations, prevents a higher score. A score of 7 reflects a solid contribution with demonstrable improvements and clear potential for future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploiting the Exact Denoising Posterior Score in Training-Free Guidance of Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13614v1)
- **Authors**: Gregory Bellchambers
- **Abstract**: The success of diffusion models has driven interest in performing conditional sampling via training-free guidance of the denoising process to solve image restoration and other inverse problems. A popular class of methods, based on Diffusion Posterior Sampling (DPS), attempts to approximate the intractable posterior score function directly. In this work, we present a novel expression for the exact posterior score for purely denoising tasks that is tractable in terms of the unconditional score function. We leverage this result to analyze the time-dependent error in the DPS score for denoising tasks and compute step sizes on the fly to minimize the error at each time step. We demonstrate that these step sizes are transferable to related inverse problems such as colorization, random inpainting, and super resolution. Despite its simplicity, this approach is competitive with state-of-the-art techniques and enables sampling with fewer time steps than DPS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the challenge of training-free conditional sampling with diffusion models, specifically focusing on image restoration tasks. The authors derive a novel, tractable expression for the exact denoising posterior score in terms of the unconditional diffusion model score. They then leverage this result to analyze and improve Diffusion Posterior Sampling (DPS) by developing a method called DPS-w, which adaptively adjusts step sizes to minimize errors at each time step during the reverse diffusion process. They demonstrate that DPS-w, despite its simplicity, performs competitively with state-of-the-art training-free guidance methods on tasks like colorization, random inpainting, and super-resolution, often with fewer sampling steps.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in deriving the tractable expression for the *exact denoising* posterior score. While existing methods approximate the *general* posterior score for conditional generation, this paper offers a specific, analytical solution for denoising. The application of this to adaptive step size adjustment in DPS-w is also novel. The proposition for inpainting score is novel and insightful.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from:
    *   **Theoretical Contribution:** Provides a clearer understanding of the denoising process within diffusion models by providing an analytical solution. This opens potential avenues for further theoretical investigations.
    *   **Practical Improvement:** DPS-w offers a potentially valuable improvement to training-free guidance. The adaptive step size approach, while simple, shows promising empirical results, suggesting it could become a practical tool for image restoration.
    *   **Analysis of DPS:** The work offers insights into the strengths and weaknesses of existing DPS methods.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Mathematical Rigor:** The derivation of the tractable posterior score appears mathematically sound (although a close review of the Appendix proofs is needed to verify completely).
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of DPS-w across several image restoration tasks and noise levels. The comparison to existing methods like DAPS and DSG is thorough.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and generally easy to follow.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code and detailed implementation information (including the use of specific FFHQ and ImageNet images).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The derived score is *only* tractable for *pure denoising* and noisy inpainting, significantly limiting its applicability directly. Generalizing to other inverse problems remains a challenge. It relies on the condition A=I in denoising and noisy inpainting with diag(d1,...,dn) A operators.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While DPS-w is competitive, it does not dramatically outperform existing methods. It's an improvement, but not a paradigm shift.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The reference to Wmax parameter is only explained in the Appendix, and is important, but the tuning/fitting requires to have a single image per dataset, which limits the robustness of the method.
    *   **Analysis of choice of the reference task:** the choice for the reference task is an important choice of hyperparameter, and it's possible that other choices (even without changing the step sizes) could improve over the existing results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's results and the tractable score could potentially inform new training-free guidance methods and provide a benchmark for evaluating approximations of posterior scores. It could also lead to more efficient sampling strategies. It remains to be seen if the theoretical contributions will lead to a broader impact on the field.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel and useful theoretical result that is thoroughly validated empirically.
